# Raphael product rules: interface

Layout, controls, menus, floating surfaces, the sidebar, navigation, colours, words on screen and the guide. Part of [the product rules](../RULES.md); a rule's ID is unique across every file.

## A dialog's rows and header keep their place

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Gate | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-UI-181 | 2026-11-24 | A row of a list is as tall as what it holds, however many rows the list has. A list capped in height scrolls once its rows no longer fit; a row never shrinks below its face and words to make room, and no row's content reaches the next row. A grid that caps its height and scrolls sizes its rows by their content (`grid-auto-rows: max-content`), because rows of the default size shrink to their buttons' min-height once they overflow. | vitest, look | hard | app/src/ui-rules.test.ts::a grid that scrolls inside a capped height sizes its rows by their content (R-UI-181); app/scripts/look/audit.test.mjs::rejects a row of a scrolling list squashed below its face and words; primary-contact-picker-long; primary-contact-picker; sidebar-tile-picker |
| R-UI-182 | 2026-11-24 | Every page of Settings and Connect apps keeps its header at the top of the dialog while the page scrolls: the close, the way back and the title stay where they started and take a press at the bottom of the longest page. Each page of Team skills (the list, a package's page, a plugin's skill and a skill alone) holds its close in a header the shared sticky rule names. | vitest, look | hard | app/src/main/skill-library.test.ts::every page of Team skills keeps its close in a header the shared sticky rule holds at the top (R-UI-182); skill-library; skill-library-open; skill-library-skill-page; skill-library-doc-alone; foot-account-settings |
## The first slime is the owner's chief of staff; Raphael is the app's name and the voice's

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-CHIEF-01 | 2026-11-24 | Nobody is made to take Raphael as their main character. Right after the welcome the first-run guide asks for the owner's first slime: its look, in the members' own slime picker starting on the first blue slime, and its name, the one thing the guide asks (the way on waits for it, Enter goes on, and the built-in name is never put in the field). That slime is member 0, the owner's chief of staff from then on, and its name and slime are the owner's as every member's are: its panel and settings page change both (its role stays the chief's, with no field), and every place that names or draws it follows them, its conversation, its cards and reminders, the lines between members, usage, calendar chips and its persona's own name. "Raphael" stays the app's name and the voice's: dictation, the bottom-right voice and quick-question surfaces, the hotkeys and the app's own messages (restart Raphael, allow Raphael the microphone) keep it, and Raphael's names still reach member 0 by @ and by asking a colleague. The first member may keep or take Raphael's name; no other member may take it or the first member's. An install from before keeps its Raphael, name and face, until the owner changes them. | vitest, pytest, look | hard | app/src/main/onboarding/mount.test.ts::its name is the owner's to give: the built-in one is never in the field, and the way on waits for one; app/src/main/onboarding/mount.test.ts::the way on keeps the name and the slime as the first member's, the chief of staff, and Enter does the same; app/src/main/members.test.ts::the first member wears the slime the owner dressed it in, everywhere: its face, its colour, its name; app/src/main/member-details.test.ts::its picture is changed here: the members' slimes, the one it wears pressed, and a picture from the PC; core/tests/members/test_raphael_settings.py::test_the_owner_names_and_dresses_the_first_bot_who_is_their_chief_of_staff; core/tests/members/test_raphael_settings.py::test_a_member_never_takes_the_name_the_owner_gave_the_first_bot_nor_the_first_bot_another_members; core/tests/members/test_raphael_settings.py::test_the_persona_calls_the_first_bot_by_the_name_the_owner_gave_it; onboarding-slime; after-onboarding; member-settings-raphael-slime |

## Host browser toolbar

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-UI-180 | 2026-11-22 | The Computer panel and enlarged host Chrome viewer draw the tab and address rows inside the rounded frame above the live page. The strip shows the favicon, clipped title and close button for each scoped tab, highlights the shown tab and scrolls horizontally without moving the add button. Narrow frames reduce tabs to their favicons and close buttons. Only cut titles carry title tooltips. The address is editable in both sizes while watching or holding input. Chrome's toolbar actions are available according to the shown tab's history. Back, Forward, Reload, Add and Close use the app's Lucide icon controls and localized accessible labels. The page fills the area below the toolbar at 1280:900 in the panel and enlarged view; the card preview keeps the same aspect. The enlarged frame fits inside the window and is centered. | vitest, look | hard | app/src/main/host-browser-toolbar.test.ts::opens, selects and closes Chrome tabs and offers history while watching; app/src/main/host-browser-toolbar.test.ts::submits the address for the displayed tab and preserves an in-progress edit through state updates; desktop-host-tabs-panel; desktop-host-tabs-expanded; desktop-host-tabs-control; desktop-host-tabs-popup |

## Toasts

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-UI-177 | 2026-11-21 | A toast never repeats a result already visible where the owner acted. Creation, deletion, successful sign-in and other visible state changes use their own rows or controls as feedback. A toast can report an unseen result or a failure, or offer recovery such as Undo. A new toast key needs a reason in the allowlist. | vitest, look | hard | app/src/main/toast-allowlist.test.ts::toast messages use only the allowlisted keys and existing message producers; member-delete; room-delete; quick-cleared; automations-delete-own; skill-library-imported |
## Switches and header controls

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Gate | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-UI-176 | | Any page or nested view with a Back control uses the member page's shared round button: 36 px square, an 18 px chevron, the shared hover surface and the keyboard focus ring. The page title stands beside it. The header's close uses the same button style (R-UI-179). | vitest, look | hard | app/src/ui-rules.test.ts::page back controls use one shared round button; skill-library-open; member-skills-library; plugins-detail; connect-apps-back-title; onboarding-back-focus |
| R-UI-178 | 2026-11-22 | A row with a switch (`.tg`) and other controls puts the switch at the far right of the visible controls. Overflow, access and navigation actions precede it. The row still owns its hover fill (R-UI-157) and right click opens its overflow menu (R-UI-44). | vitest, look | hard | app/scripts/look/audit.test.mjs::rejects a visible control to the right of a switch; skill-library; skill-library-open; connect-app-connected; member-settings-skills |
| R-UI-179 | 2026-11-22 | A header's close × uses the same shared round 36 px button, frame, rim and 18 px glyph as Back (R-UI-176). With both controls visible, their vertical centres are at most 1 px apart: the back button stands alone on the header's first row and the title on the row below. Without a back button the close shares the title's centre line. On Connectors' list of the connected apps (已連結) and a plugin's page in Team skills the way back is the title itself (an app's page and the MCP form keep the round Back): a plain 18 px chevron and the title words, plain text with no circle or fill, both pressable, dimmed at rest and full under the pointer; the close then shares the title's centre line. In Settings, Connect apps and the member and connection dialogs the close stands alone in a column at the top right: the body keeps that column free, so nothing of the dialog runs under the close. A bare × glyph is forbidden in any dialog header. Window caption controls follow R-UI-16. | vitest, look | hard | app/scripts/look/audit.test.mjs::rejects an unframed or misaligned header close and a bare close glyph; connect-apps; connect-apps-connected; connectors-slack-detail; skill-library-open; member-maker; model-choices-local |

## Local command completion

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-PC-07 | | A local command on Windows or macOS, including a background job, completes when its shell exits. Capture stdout and stderr for up to one more second, then return the shell's exit code and keep its surviving descendants running. Timeout or cancellation terminates the command's process tree. A real timeout returns partial output with `timed_out: true` and a null exit code. | pytest | hard | core/tests/computer/test_local_commands.py::test_an_exited_local_command_leaves_its_pipe_holding_descendant_running; core/tests/computer/test_local_commands.py::test_a_local_command_timeout_kills_its_tree |

A written-form submenu closes before its anchor is redrawn. | vitest, look | hard | app/src/main/pages/settings-providers.test.ts::pressing a spoken language redraws the picker and Settings row with the variants submenu open: %s; dictation-language-untick |

## Choosing people shows their faces, and a choice is never a box on the left

Who may use a package of 團隊技能 was a column of bare names, each with its check at the left, and Safety chose whom an entry is for (and which entries of an imported file to keep) with the browser's own check boxes: with many members the names alone are hard to tell apart, and a small box at the left does not show at a glance who is chosen.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-UI-174 | | A list or menu that chooses members leads each row with the member's face as the window draws it everywhere (members.ts `faceHTML`, from the roster: its slime or its picture; without the roster, its id's own colour) and puts the check, when chosen, at the row's right edge: never a check or a box at the left. 團隊技能's 誰可以使用 (skill-library.ts) is the shared menu's row: a 20 px face (`.mlead`), the name, the check's place at the right (`.ck`); 整個團隊 leads with the team's own picture, its first four faces in one square, Raphael first, as the sidebar draws the team (`groupFacesHTML`). Ticking names keeps the menu open, and ticking the last one or 整個團隊 closes it, as before. In 安全 (vault.ts) whom an entry is for and which entries an imported file brings in are rows with no box, the group dialog's `.room-row`: each row one button with `aria-pressed`, a member's 28 px face and name (an entry's label over its site) at the left and the round check at the right edge, and a chosen row is filled the whole way with the selected colour (`--selected`, on every pressed `.room-row`, the group dialogs' too). The rows stand on the pane, not on a tinted list, so the fill shows in both themes; a press chooses or leaves out a row where it stands and keeps the private fields typed above it. | vitest, look | hard | app/src/main/skill-library.test.ts::each row of the access menu leads with who it names, a face or the team's own picture, and its check stands at the right; app/src/main/skill-library.test.ts::the access menu still opens with a face on every row when the roster cannot be read; app/src/main/skill-library.test.ts::the access menu ticks characters one by one and turns into the whole team when all are ticked; app/src/main/vault.test.ts::lists the members to choose as rows with no box: the face on the left, pressed and checked at the right when chosen; app/src/main/vault.test.ts::draws an edited entry's chosen members as chosen rows; app/src/main/vault.test.ts::confirms only checked preview entries and cancels transient previews on close; skill-library-access; vault-sharing; vault-import-preview; plus-create-room |

## A Settings section's name stands on its card's edge

Every small section title of Settings (使用的模型, 訂閱帳號, 這個月, 方案額度, 花費紀錄…) stood inset like the rows of its card, while the card and the controls under it (7 天 / 30 天) stood on the card's outer edge: two left edges in one section.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-UI-172 | 2026-11-21 | Every section of Settings, on every pane, has its small name on the left edge of the card under it, not inset like the card's content: the shared `.modal-body .sgroup > h2` (and `.data-heading`'s, and 方案額度's `.run-allowance` header) has no left margin or padding, and a line under the name (這個月's) or the empty line standing for a card keeps that edge too. Controls between the name and the card (分析's 7 天 / 30 天, 分類方式, 依模型 / 依來源) start on the same edge with the card's own air under the name (8 px). Done once in the shared style, never per page. | vitest, look | hard | app/src/ui-rules.test.ts::a Settings section's title stands on its card's left edge: no stylesheet insets it like the card's content; look `content-width` (every Settings scene: profile, appearance, tools, skill-library, safety, models, usage, settings-usage-overview, settings-usage-analytics, dictation, about) |

## 用量 is its own tab, with 概覽 and 分析

What was spent, the monthly limit and the charts of a period were hidden in a view of 模型與用量, so the owner could not see where the money went or set the limit at all.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-USE-01 | | Settings' 模型與用量 is two tabs: 模型 (the models in use, the subscription accounts and the providers, shown directly with no pill tabs inside) and, right after it, 用量 (the gauge of the account menu's 用量), whose two small tabs are 概覽 and 分析 (ui.ts `tabs()`, as the models page had). A link to 用量 (the account menu, the report's 查看用量) opens 用量 at 概覽, and every opening of Settings starts there; 概覽 and 分析 are tabs, not steps: Back closes Settings and Forward reopens it on the same tab. 概覽 is 這個月 (the app's spending with API keys this month, in a card) and then 方案額度 (each subscription's card: its name and account, and for each window its name, how long until it resets, what is left and a bar; OpenRouter's key limit; a service whose balance cannot be read says so, ChatGPT with 管理用量). | vitest | hard | app/src/main/pages/settings-view.test.ts::are ordered General, Models, Usage, Tools, Team skills, Dictation, Safety and About; app/src/main/pages/settings-view.test.ts::puts providers and model choices on Models with no views inside, and this month's spending and the allowances on Usage; app/src/main/pages/settings-view.test.ts::用量 has two small tabs, 概覽 and 分析, drawn with the shared tabs, and 分析 draws its period's sections; app/src/main/pages/settings-providers.test.ts::models opens Models with no tabs inside and no saved fallback settings, and usage opens its own tab at 概覽; app/src/main/pages/settings-providers.test.ts::the shared tabs support arrow keys, and Settings has no search; app/src/main/pages/settings-providers.test.ts::Back closes Settings and Forward restores the selected Usage tab with %s; app/src/main/usage-analytics.test.ts::each window is named, says how long until it resets and what is left over its bar, and the card names its plan |
| R-USE-02 | | The gear (每月上限) at the top right of 這個月's card opens a small popover of the app's own: 不設上限 and 設上限 as two tabs (the owner chose them over a dropdown: the choice is seen at once; the exception R-UI-20 names) and, when set, a US$ field. A change is stored at once with no save button: 不設上限 stores null, an amount is stored when it is typed (Enter, leaving the field, or closing the popover), and what is not a number is said under the field and stored nowhere. ## A diagram opens from its chart, and its larger view zooms and moves

Only the small icon at the top right of a reply's diagram card opened it larger, and the larger view could neither zoom nor move, so a wide flow chart stayed too small to read.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-UI-173 | | A drawn diagram's chart, the whole area under its card's header, is one control named 放大檢視圖表 that opens the larger view as the header's expand icon does: a press anywhere on it, or Enter or Space on it (one Tab stop, only once the chart is drawn; before that the source stands there and nothing is pressed). The header's copy, source and expand icons keep their own actions, and a press that moved (a drag, words of the chart being selected) opens nothing. Under the pointer the chart shows the pointer and one calm grey step laid over the whole chart, a veil over the drawing so its nodes keep their contrast in both themes; no frame lights and no tooltip shows. The larger view is the shared dialog: its × and Escape close it and it keeps no footer (R-UI-117). The chart stands on the ground it was drawn on, the conversation's panel, never the dialog's lighter one (on it the dark theme's nodes sank into the ground). It starts fitted in the middle of its view, with air around it and at most twice its own size; the mouse wheel or a trackpad's pinch zooms it about the pointer, from a quarter of its fitted size to four times it (and always up to twice its own size, so a large chart's words can be read); a drag with the left, middle or right button moves it, with no menu, selection or scrolling, and some of it always stays in view; a double press puts it back. The head holds zoom out, zoom in and reset (−, + and the corner icon) before the ×, the same round buttons on its row; − and + are disabled at the limits, and from the keyboard the arrows move the chart and + - 0 zoom and reset it. The chart is drawn at each size, never a scaled picture of it, so it stays sharp. One quiet line under the chart says how to use it in the window's language (滾輪縮放，按住拖曳移動，雙擊重設), with no tooltip. | vitest, look | hard | app/src/main/message-rich.test.ts::a press anywhere on a drawn diagram's chart opens it larger, and the header's icons keep their own actions; app/src/main/message-rich.test.ts::a press that moved, or that selected words of the chart, does not open the diagram; app/src/main/message-rich.test.ts::Enter or Space on the focused chart opens the diagram larger, and other keys do not; app/src/main/diagram-zoom.test.ts::the larger diagram starts fitted in the middle of its view, and zooms from a quarter to four times that size; app/src/main/diagram-zoom.test.ts::the wheel zooms about the pointer, a pinch follows the fingers, and a sideways scroll moves the chart across; app/src/main/diagram-zoom.test.ts::dragging with the left, middle or right button moves the chart with the pointer, and some of it always stays in view; app/src/main/diagram-zoom.test.ts::a double press, the reset control and the 0 key put the chart back fitted, and the arrows and + - move and zoom it; app/src/main/diagram-zoom.test.ts::pressing the chart opens the larger view: −, + and reset before the ×, and one line under it that says how to use it in the window's language; rich-diagram-hover; rich-diagram-press; rich-diagram-key; rich-diagram-zoomed; rich-expand |

## The status light in place of the search button, and a title pill without a dot

With twenty members and folded groups nobody could see who was working, the conversation's search button went unused, and the pill's dot meant nothing to the owner.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-HEAD-01 | | The left of a member conversation's top row is the status list (status-light.ts), not a search button: one icon (steps), whose name and tooltip say who (沒有人在工作, 小森在等你, 小晴等 2 位正在工作, 小葵完成了). A 6 px dot stays at the icon's top right while any listed conversation has unopened finished work or an unseen waiting card. Its colour comes from the most urgent unseen item (waiting, failed, finished). Opening the menu leaves every item unseen. Opening a conversation from the status list, the sidebar or another route clears its finished news and acknowledges its current waiting cards. A card still waiting for an answer stays in the list. New work or a new card can light the icon again. Work under way never lights the icon. A press lists in the shared menu, each with a still coloured dot and its face, those waiting for the owner or whose work went wrong under 等你 (the warning or the error colour), those whose finished work the owner has not opened under 完成 (the success colour), and finally those working under 工作中 (the text's colour), nothing checked; picking one opens its conversation, which takes it out of 完成. ## Every app wears its own mark, at one size

The owner's 工具 tab of a member showed the plug for 「Google」 and Outlook visibly larger than Notion, GitHub and the other marks of the list. Causes: the Google account had no mark of its own (other places borrowed Gmail's, the 工具 tab fell to the plug that stands for any unknown app), and the marks' box (`.mono`, 28 x 22) let a picture's percentage height go unresolved: an inline mark was drawn 22 px while a catalogue picture with its own 128 px size took the box's 28 px width. Now there is one mark for the Google account, one generic mark for every app nobody drew, and one square for every mark, and a rule test fails on an app without a mark, on the plug standing for an app and on a stylesheet that draws a mark wider than tall.

## An address in words read whole is a link

An all-day item a member had synced from another app into the calendar held a page's address in its notes, and the detail card drew it as dead text: only the words of a message were links. Now one helper draws plain words with their addresses as links wherever words are read whole, and one listener opens every link of the window.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-LINK-01 | 2026-11-16 | Every http or https address in words the window shows whole (the notes of the calendar's detail card, a conversation card's words and a question card's title, a routine's instructions, a sent mail and the message a permission card would send, a connection's technical reason, a room's decisions, a template's words, a connected step's result, the release notes and a connected app's description) is a link made by the one helper `linkedText` (message-content.ts, which shares `bareUrl` with the message renderer): every character is escaped first, the link's words are the address itself as written with no tooltip, and it ends at a space, a quote, CJK punctuation, a full stop, comma or bracket that closes the sentence, and at Chinese, Japanese or Korean words set right after it unless they start a part of the address (`/`, `=`, `?`, `#`, `&` or `@` before them); `javascript:`, `data:`, `file:`, bare `www.` and an address with a sign-in in it stay words. A press, a Ctrl or Shift press and a middle press open it in the owner's browser through the one capture listener `installLinkOpener` (link-opener.ts, started in main.ts), never the window and never the row, card or panel around it, with the shell's `open_external_url` the only gate; a right press keeps the app's own menu. Words in a field being edited, a one-line preview cut short and words inside a button stay plain. | vitest, look | hard | app/src/main/text-links.test.ts::a press opens the address once in the browser and never the row around it, which a press beside the link still opens; app/src/main/text-links.test.ts::Ctrl, Shift and a middle click open it the same way, and a right click is left to the app's menu; app/src/main/text-links.test.ts::the calendar's detail card links the address in its notes, opens it once and stays open; app/src/main/text-links.test.ts::Chinese, Japanese or Korean words right after an address end it, unless they start a part of the address; app/src/main/text-links.test.ts::javascript:, data:, file:, a bare www. and an address with a sign-in in it are never links; app/src/main/text-links.test.ts::text that is edited stays text: the calendar's form keeps the address in its field |

## A conversation read at its bottom stays at its bottom

When a member replied, the thread jumped up a little from its bottom. Cause: following the bottom was a correction queued by the thread's own draws, so a height change no draw made was not followed: a picture the core read back late (a member's picture, the large pictures of a conversation just opened), and the same picture standing empty for a frame each time its line was drawn again, left the thread a few to some hundreds of pixels above its bottom, and a growth past 80 px also made it stop following.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-FOLLOW-01 | 2026-11-14 | A conversation the owner reads at its bottom (private, Raphael's or a room's) stays at its bottom in every painted frame, whatever changes its height or its scroller's: their message, the work line and the slime, the words, pictures and cards that come late, the end of the work, paced bubbles, reactions, the composer and what stands under the thread, and a conversation that opens with pictures still on their way. One place keeps it there (message-rich.ts `readingAnchor`): a ResizeObserver on the scroller and its content writes the bottom in the frame of the change, while the thread follows. A picture drawn again when its line changes takes the copy already loaded at once and never stands empty. The owner's own scrolling up (the wheel, also over the header, the scroll keys, a touch) lets go of the bottom before it moves anything; from then on nothing moves the line they read, however near the bottom, until they are back at the very bottom or press the latest button; a press on the floating row of a waiting question (R-OWNER-03) is their own move to that question, in a room as in a member's conversation. No timer and no smooth scrolling hide a jump. | vitest, look | hard | app/src/main/message-rich.test.ts::a thread at its bottom stays there through every change of its height or its scroller's, in the frame of the change; app/src/main/message-rich.test.ts::a thread the owner scrolled up stays where they read, near the bottom or far from it, until they are back at its bottom; app/src/main/message-rich.test.ts::a scroll still going up does not take the bottom again, a thread that cannot scroll is not let go, and the latest button follows; app/src/main/attachment-media.test.ts::a picture drawn again takes the copy already loaded at once and never stands empty; app/scripts/look/follow-probe.test.mjs::fails a picture already drawn that stands shorter again, while a picture still on its way only grows; app/src/main/message-rich.test.ts::a thread the owner scrolled up that comes to its bottom by getting shorter follows again; app/src/main/conversation-scroll.test.ts::the thread hears a wheel turned over the header before it moves, so a wheel up there lets go of the bottom too (R-FOLLOW-01); follow-reply-near; follow-reply-bottom; follow-reply-reading; follow-open-pictures; owner-question-brought; owner-question-pending-room |
| R-FOLLOW-02 | 2026-11-18 | A conversation read in the middle keeps the line read when its column changes width: the details panel dragged, opened or closed, the window resized. That scroll is the width change's, not the owner's: the place read before it is put back (reading-place.ts) and stays theirs (`conversation-hold` to message-rich.ts `readingAnchor`), so the thread does not start following the newest turn; a thread read at its bottom stays at its bottom. | vitest, look | hard | app/src/main/reading-place.test.ts::a wider, shorter thread clamped to its new end before the width is reported keeps the place read in the middle, and says the owner holds it; app/src/main/message-rich.test.ts::a place in the middle that a width change put back is the owner's: the end in view for a moment does not make the thread follow; panel-close-keeps-place; panel-drag-keeps-place |
| R-FOLLOW-03 | 2026-11-18 | On a Mac, a conversation kept at its bottom is painted afresh after it grows: WebKit left the whole thread black, under a normal header and composer, when a member's reply landed (the longer, the surer: a long one all black) or its work ended, until a scroll. ## Long work tells the owner how it goes

A member said nothing of its progress: a hand-off task of a member on Claude Code ran 37 minutes with 71 tool calls and wrote 142 steps and not one message until the owner stopped it, so the owner could not tell whether it had crashed. Cause: what a member says while it works is progress in the folded work (`_release_held`, only one opening is a bubble), and the API rules keep `messages` for an important result; nothing ever asked a working member for a word.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-PROGRESS-01 | 2026-11-14 | A run the owner reads as it works (a turn of the owner's own conversation and a task in a member's private conversation, on API and CLI brains) keeps the time of its last bubble (`agent/progress_updates.py`; the opening counts, a progress line does not). When a tool result comes back 120 s into a run with no bubble, or 300 s after the last, it carries one line for the model (FIRST_UPDATE_S and NEXT_UPDATE_S, in one place): it has been N minutes since the owner was told anything, so before the next step write a two-sentence update (what is done, what is next) in plain words with no tool names; an API conversation turn is told to put it in `messages`, which NARRATION_RULES now allow for an update a reminder asks for. The line goes after the result and outside its untrusted wrapper (a CLI's MCP result, `ToolOutcome.note` in the worker loop, `host.tool`'s `note` for a background API task); stored tool events, CLI receipts and the CLI's tool conversation keep the real result. One reminder until the next bubble, said again only after another 300 s. While the run is past that time, what it says next is a bubble and not progress: a CLI's held or plain speech between steps (foreground, and a task's through its `_reply`), a background API task's words with its calls. Unchanged: the opening, the final answer, the folded work, routines (the owner reads only the last message), a meeting's reply (its words are its post), consultations, a colleague's question, greetings and memory passes, which keep no clock. The default prompt stays inside its budget (the default prompt test: before 16024 / 15202 tokens for an API / CLI brain, after 16023 / 15202, limits 16048 / 15212). | pytest | hard | core/tests/agent/test_progress_updates.py::test_the_first_word_is_owed_after_two_minutes_and_the_next_five_minutes_after_the_last; core/tests/agent/test_progress_updates.py::test_a_run_that_told_the_owner_something_lately_is_asked_for_nothing; core/tests/agent/test_progress_updates.py::test_a_cli_turn_is_asked_for_a_word_at_two_minutes_then_five_and_its_update_is_a_message; core/tests/agent/test_progress_updates.py::test_a_reminder_nobody_answered_comes_again_only_after_five_minutes; core/tests/agent/test_progress_updates.py::test_a_cli_turn_whose_opening_was_shown_lately_is_asked_for_nothing_yet; core/tests/agent/test_progress_updates.py::test_a_cli_conversation_turn_gets_the_reminder_and_shows_its_update; core/tests/agent/test_progress_updates.py::test_an_api_conversation_turn_is_asked_for_an_update_in_messages; core/tests/background/test_progress_updates.py::test_an_api_task_is_asked_for_a_word_and_its_update_reaches_the_owner; core/tests/background/test_progress_updates.py::test_a_cli_task_is_asked_for_a_word_and_its_update_reaches_reply; core/tests/members/test_management_discovery.py::test_default_production_prompt_stays_below_the_measured_pre_unit_budget |

## Our own media players, and media that load once

The app shows its own media (pictures, pages, HTML, video) with its own players, not the system's defaults, and caches whatever can be cached, so that opening a conversation does not load everything again. The inventory of every kind and how each is shown is in features/conversation.md (Our own media players).

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-MEDIA-01 | 2026-11-15 | Every stored video and sound in the app (a member's media message, the owner's own file, a room's, an older URL card, the full screen) plays in our own player (`media-player.ts`), never the browser's: the element has no `controls`, carries `controlslist="nodownload nofullscreen noremoteplayback noplaybackrate"`, `disablepictureinpicture` and `disableremoteplayback`, and a right click opens no native menu (the app's own right click, context-menu.ts). A video plays and pauses on a press on its picture, on Space or K anywhere in the player but on another button (Space presses that button), and on its play button. | R-MEDIA-03 | 2026-11-15 | Cause of the loading on every open: each picture of a conversation crossed IPC as base64 (`attachments.get`, decoded with `atob` into a Blob) every time the conversation was opened, because the thread's `reset()` revoked every Blob URL when it was left; videos and sounds went through Tauri's asset protocol, which sends no cache headers and names a disk path. Now a stored picture, video or sound comes by its opaque id from the shell's own protocol (`media_protocol.rs`; `http://raphael-media.localhost/<id>` on Windows): `attachment_source` asks the core (`attachments.path`, the same reviewed boundary, now with the stored type) and grants that one file to the main window; the protocol answers only the main window, only an id granted in this run, only raster pictures, video and audio, with `Cache-Control: private, max-age=31536000, immutable`, `ETag: "<id>"`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, 304 for a matching If-None-Match and byte ranges of at most 2 MiB. A stored id names its bytes for good (written once under a fresh id, never rewritten), so the URL carries no per-session token: the grant is checked on the shell's side in each run and the cache key stays the same from one run to the next. The window keeps each id's URL for the session (`media-cache.ts`: asked once, whoever asks, at the same time or later) and holds up to 120 drawn pictures (192 MB decoded), so a conversation opened again sets its pictures in the same frame and asks the shell for nothing; an id the protocol does not serve falls back to `attachments.get` once, held as a Blob of bounded size. Measured in look (`media-reopen`, two pictures of about 1.7 MB, both themes at 1120, 720 and 1920): before, every open read both as 4,494,936 base64 characters over IPC, and the reopen painted in 389 to 460 ms on a quiet machine and 700 to 844 ms on a busier one; after, measured right after the busier run, the first open makes 2 grants and 2 protocol requests (3.37 MB) and the reopen no call and no request, painted in 110 to 237 ms. Nothing that changes (a page being edited, a computer's frame) uses this cache. | vitest, look, pytest, cargo test | hard | app/src/main/media-cache.test.ts::an id's URL is asked of the shell once a session, whoever asks and however often; app/src/main/media-cache.test.ts::a failed ask is not remembered: the next one asks again; app/src/main/media-cache.test.ts::pictures kept decoded are bounded by count and by decoded size, the oldest going first; app/src/main/attachment-media.test.ts::a stored picture comes by its id from the media protocol, once a session, and a thread opened again draws it at once; app/src/main/attachment-media.test.ts::two threads that ask for the same picture at the same time ask the shell once; app/src/main/attachment-media.test.ts::a stored copy the protocol does not serve is read once as a Blob instead; app/src/csp.test.ts::stored attachments load from the app's own media protocol as pictures and media only, never as scripts, frames or connections; core/tests/services/test_attachment_upload.py::test_the_path_of_a_stored_file_names_the_type_it_was_stored_as; app/src-tauri/src/media_protocol.rs (cargo test: a_whole_file_comes_with_the_cache_headers_and_exact_bytes, a_copy_the_browser_holds_is_not_sent_again, one_response_carries_at_most_two_mebibytes, what_was_not_granted_or_is_not_an_id_is_not_found, only_pictures_video_and_sound_are_granted); app/src-tauri/src/commands.rs (cargo test: an_attachment_is_granted_to_the_media_protocol_not_the_asset_protocol); features/conversation.md (media-reopen, generated-image, picture-message) |

## The interface language is the window's alone

The interface language selects interface text only: it is told to no model and decides no reply language. This replaces the configured reply language every external turn and every background task was told (`reply_context`, 「Owner's configured reply language: …」) and the interface locale every system prompt named (`interface_context`, 「Interface locale: … This selects fixed UI labels only.」), and supersedes R-AG-LANG-01's "interface metadata" in features/conversation.md.

## Reordering the sidebar follows the pointer

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-SIDE-03 | 2026-11-14 | Reordering the sidebar stalled. Measured, a drop sent one members.update per member of the folder (or one groups.update per folder), each waited for the core's answer (about 16 ms of its own work on a fresh database with fifteen members, `sync_homes` writing every member's home), each announced a change that read the whole roster again and drew the panel, the settings page and the conversation again: the row stood where it was dropped about 370 ms later in the mock core, three reads and five saves for one move. Now a row or a folder stands where it is dropped in the same task (`orderMs` under 150 ms, measured 10 to 60 ms), saved with one request (`members.update {group_id?, position}` or `groups.update {position}`; `position` is a place among every member but Raphael, `movedMember` in member-order.ts numbering as store.py `_move` does, pinned in core/tests/members/test_service.py). Only the moved row is a new node (and only when it changes folder: its face is kept); the rows and faces beside it, the folders and the conversation are the same nodes before and after. Moves wait for each other, and a read begun before a move or while it is saved is not drawn over it (R-SLIME-02). The core's answer and its change event that only confirm the order draw nothing but the column's patch (`withoutPlaces`: places, folders, the roster's order and revision are left out of the comparison, so no panel, settings page, conversation or Library read follows). A save that fails puts the column back, says why in the toast, and reads the core again. A move to where the row stands asks nothing; the hidden folder takes no drop. | vitest, pytest, look | hard | app/src/main/member-order.test.ts; app/src/main/pages/agent-members.test.ts::a row or a folder dropped on the sidebar; core/tests/members/test_service.py::test_one_update_puts_a_member_in_a_folder_at_a_place_among_all_members_and_numbers_them_again; sidebar-reorder-members; sidebar-reorder-across; sidebar-reorder-folder; app/scripts/look/drag-probe.mjs; app/scripts/look/drag-probe.test.mjs; app/scripts/look/mock-ipc.test.mjs |

## The sidebar's lights

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-SIDE-04 | 2026-11-14 | A member that was only thinking showed a light with nothing to read behind it, and the lights said too little of what each member needed. Cause: the core marked a thread unread at every `agent.turn` event of a turn that is written, and a running turn is written when it begins and again at each step (a routine's too, the moment it starts), so a member that only thought had a dot and nothing to read behind it. Now a turn that is still running marks its thread only once it has sent a message (a `reply`; a progress line is none), and the roster says what the unseen news is (`members.list` `unread_kind`, `rooms.list` `unread_kind`: `message`; `done` for a turn of a task or a routine that ended well, and for a group chat whose meeting ended with words the owner has not read; `failed` for a turn or task that ended in error, expiry, a limit or an interruption, the owner's own no or stop excepted; `failed` outranks `done` outranks `message`). A sidebar row (a member, a group chat, a pinned face, on the rail the face's corner) wears one still dot, the strongest of: orange (`--wait`, a question or an approval waits for the owner: `waiting` or the activity `blocked`; it stays until it is answered), red (`--err`, failed, or a run that failed unseen), green (`--ok`, done), white (`--text`, the theme's own foreground, a new message), none (nothing new: a member that thinks or works says nothing, its slime shows the work). The dot keeps the size and place it had (`--pa-dot`), and keeps its place under the pointer: a row draws no 「more」 (its right click is the menu, R-UI-44), so nothing makes room for one and the light and the words stand where every row's do (the row used to widen its right padding for the hidden button). It never animates, has no tooltip (its words would repeat the row) and names itself for a screen reader: 等你處理, 需要你看一下, 已完成, 有新訊息. Opening the conversation reads white, green and red at once (`lightOf(row, open)`; the core clears them with `members.read`), and orange stays while its question waits. The four colours keep 3:1 on every surface a row stands on, selected, hovered and pressed too (check-palette.mjs `sidebarLights`). The header's member picker shows one dot for all the other members, the strongest of their lights. | vitest, pytest, look | hard | app/src/main/sidebar-light.test.ts::a member that is thinking or working has none, nothing new to read; app/src/main/sidebar-light.test.ts::opening the conversation reads white, green and red, and an orange question stays while it waits; core/tests/members/test_service.py::test_a_member_that_is_thinking_is_not_news_until_it_has_sent_something; core/tests/members/test_service.py::test_a_routine_that_starts_is_not_news_and_one_that_ends_is_done; core/tests/members/test_service.py::test_a_failed_turn_is_failed_news_and_a_reply_after_it_does_not_hide_it; core/tests/members/test_service.py::test_unread_kind_ranks_failed_over_done_over_message; core/tests/members/test_rooms.py::test_a_room_says_what_its_news_is_and_a_finished_meeting_is_done; sidebar-lights; sidebar-lights-rail; sidebar-lights-pinned; sidebar-lights-hover; sidebar-lights-hover-room; sidebar-lights-hover-pinned; sidebar-lights-thinking; sidebar-lights-open; sidebar-unread; app/scripts/check-palette.mjs |
| R-SIDE-05 | 2026-11-17 | Words the owner writes in a composer and has not sent yet stand in that conversation's sidebar row: its second line reads 草稿：<the words> (`members.draft`, one line as `plainLine` makes it) in place of the last line, for a member (its main conversation's draft, else another of its conversations') and for a group chat alike, the open conversation's own row too. They come with the draft as it is kept (`writeDraftWords`, 300 ms after the last key) and go when it goes (sent, emptied, given up); a quote alone or spaces are no words. | R-SIDE-06 | 2026-11-17 | A member's sidebar line is the owner's message only until the member says something back. ## Owner-PC commands

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-PC-01 | 2026-11-13 | The owner's own screen (the run on their desktop, the fixed commands that open, lock, copy and change the volume, and the fast router that picks those) is in reach of a quick question and of nothing else: not an ordinary conversation, typed or said, Raphael's or a member's, not a room, an exchange between members, a message from a phone, Telegram or Slack, a task or a routine, and no permission mode, 全部允許 included, reopens it. Such a turn is offered none of those tools, the fast router is not asked to route it, and a call that names one anyway is refused. What it shows on a screen (a website, YouTube, a program, clicks, typing) it shows on the member's own computer, and when that is not available the member says so and never falls back to the owner's screen. In a quick question the owner's PC is the default for everything, commands and what is seen alike, and the member's own computer is offered beside it for a request whose words name it (用你的電腦, your computer, in any language): the model reads the owner's words and picks the tool, the prompt says which is the default and how the owner names the other, and no word matched in code takes either computer away (such words only skip the fast router's shortcut, which can use the owner's PC alone). Public paraphrases of early product decisions. Later rules take precedence. Each row is one requirement. Explicit conflicts remain `human` with both versions. `hard` violations fail judge regardless of score; `human` needs product/native acceptance. This ledger records requirements, not claims of compliance. Mechanical references use `repository/path::exact test title`.

Home no longer exists (R-UI-114, R-UI-43, R-UI-03 and R-UI-04 are retired): the app opens on Raphael's
conversation and the calendar opens only when asked (R-NAV-02, R-SIDE-02). The designs of Home below are
kept only as the ledger's history.

| R-VC-02 | 2026-10-31 | The dictation key works while a question said into a conversation is answered; only the recording itself holds the microphone. | test | hard | app/src-tauri/src/dictation.rs::dictation_works_while_a_question_said_in_a_conversation_is_answered |
| R-VC-03 | 2026-11-11 | The dictation key always wins. Once the words of a question said to the overlay (Alt+Space) are in, it goes on as a turn of its own, so a standalone Right Alt starts a dictation while its answer is worked on, waits for a yes or no, or is on the overlay, exactly as if nothing were there; the model call is never cancelled by it, and stopping the question stays what it was (Esc or the overlay's Stop, which also drops an answer that waits). While the owner dictates the overlay shows the dictation alone: an answer that arrives meanwhile, or was showing when the recording began, is held, and once the dictation is finished and its words are typed it is shown for its ordinary time. If a newer quick question was asked meanwhile only the newest answer shows; every answer is in the quick conversation as ever. A held answer is kept in memory only, so it is dropped when the app quits or the core restarts. Right Alt stays ignored only while the question itself is recorded or transcribed (the core has one session at a time), and Alt+Space does not bury a yes or no question that waits. ## Connect apps in one view, and one colour for everything that floats

Connect Apps is one view, not separate connected and store tabs: the connected apps stand above the recommended ones, the recommended ones are filtered by category (all, communication, productivity, development), and no count of the catalogue is shown. Every floating surface, a dialog included, follows one set of colours.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-FLOAT-01 | 2026-11-12 | Everything that floats over the window (a dialog, a menu and its submenu, a popover, a floating card, a tooltip, a toast) stands on one surface with one edge, in the dark theme: the window and sidebar are #0e0e0e, what floats is #1f1f1f (`--float`) with a 1 px #2b2b2b edge (`--float-edge`), except a dialog, which is the darker middle layer (`--dialog`, R-FLOAT-03); a control standing on it (a chip, a 連結 button, a field, a card inside) is filled #282828 (`--float-ctl`, hovered #303030), a hovered menu row is #292929 (`--float-hover`), a divider inside is the edge colour, the chosen chip is a light fill with dark words (`--float-sel`, `--float-on-sel`), and the dimmed window behind a dialog reads about #050505. The light theme keeps its character: a white surface, a #e5e5e5 edge, a light grey control (#ececec) and hover (#eaeaea), a black chosen chip with white words. The surfaces say it once (`:where(.menu, .modal, .mb-dialog, ...)` in styles.css points the ordinary tokens at the floating ones inside them), `--menu` is `var(--float)`, and a new floating surface joins that list and uses `--float` and `--float-edge`. Pages, the sidebar, the thread and the composer keep their own colours. A toast at the window's foot floats too: a soft pill on `--float` with its edge, its words in the text colour and a button framed by the control fill, never the inverse of the page (the white box it was on the dark theme). Every text grey keeps 4.5:1 on `--float`, `--float-ctl` and their hover and pressed fills, the status colours and the delete question's red hold on them (check-palette.mjs), and a text button on a floating surface is framed by its fill (R-UI-18). | vitest, lint, look | hard | app/src/main/floating.test.ts::every floating surface stands on the floating tokens and says it in one scope; app/src/main/floating.test.ts::the tokens are fixed greys in the dark theme and a white surface in the light one; scripts/check-palette.mjs; connect-apps; connect-apps-more; member-delete-ask; context-menu-member; composer-model-small; member-maker; automations-detail-own; model-choices-answer; profile |

| R-UI-121 | 2026-11-11 | A press outside an open menu, popover, picker or card closes it and is delivered to what was pressed, in the same press, as if nothing had been open; it is never spent on closing. A dialog with a scrim is the exception below. A menu or popover lets the press through untouched (its own pointerdown, focus and click), and a drawer's scrim (the narrow sidebar, the details panel) closes its panel and then gives the element under the pointer the focus and one click, once. A dialog's scrim (Connect apps, Settings, the member creator, the model chooser, the delete question) only closes it and the press is spent: nothing behind it is pressed, focused or right-clicked, and no selection starts. A menu open over a dialog is closed first and the dialog by the next press, since the scrim hides the page until then. Presses that began inside the panel, a selection dragged out of it, and a press the panel may not close right now (a busy dialog, a calendar editor holding typed words) close nothing and press nothing. A right click outside a menu or popover closes it and opens the menu of what it pressed, if that has one; over a dialog's scrim it only closes the dialog; an 'are you sure' step is abandoned by any outside press, which still goes through. The focus follows the pressed element; Escape and the keyboard are unchanged. One policy in dismissals.ts serves every dialog, menu and popover. The one control that is not pressed is the one that opened the thing, which only closes it (R-UI-124). A frame (a page open in the conversation's place, a computer's screen) is another document, so a press inside it gives the app no pointer event: the app takes the frame receiving the focus with the pointer over it for that press, and closes every panel the frame is outside of while the frame keeps the press. | vitest, look | hard | app/src/main/dismissals.test.ts::a press on a control outside an open popover closes it and the control's handler runs once, with the focus on it; | R-REACT-01 | 2026-11-14 | A message carries at most one reaction of the owner's, where it is stored: the primary key is the message (migration 82 kept the newest of a message that had several), and choosing another emoji replaces it in one write. Every reaction is analysed, at once: any reaction of the owner's to a reply of a member's own, positive or negative (Raphael too; in a group room only the reacted message's author), starts that member's own turn at once, with no wait and no list of emoji that count: `source` reaction, empty question, started by an internal note that names the emoji and holds the reply, the owner's message and the few turns before as data, on the member's own model with no tool, no other model to fall back on and no message of the owner's. It shows at once as the member's usual presence in the conversation (「思考中」). The member decides, in its own voice and in the owner's language (the window's language is named in the prompt and wins over what the member teaches or the conversation holds, unless the owner's own last words are in another; the reaction prompt carries no rule that lets a persona choose, and the task comes after the persona; the rule for an ordinary reply, `REPLY_LANGUAGE`, no longer follows an "established conversation": it answers in the language of the owner's latest message, not a lesson's): ask in one or two sentences what was wrong when the emoji shows dissatisfaction (to say quickly what they are unhappy with), say a few words (a brief acknowledgement, the obvious next step), or say nothing, and then the turn is taken out of the thread: no message, card, failure or notice shows that it ran. Nothing is a pass: the member answers exactly (pass), as a member does in a group room that has nothing to add (`is_pass`: any case, brackets, a full stop or bold). The answer format has no field for silence, and the loop sends an empty answer back for words, so the room's own pass is reused rather than a new token. Cost: every reaction is one model call on that member's model (accepted by the owner). A reaction taken back, or changed to another emoji, while its turn runs ends that turn and leaves nothing (a changed one starts the new emoji's), and so does the owner writing in that conversation (their message is not held up). A reaction is analysed once per message and emoji (`reaction_asks`, keyed by kind, message and emoji since migration 83): the same emoji given again is not analysed again, another emoji is; one that was ended or failed was never made, so it is analysed when it is given again. A member working for the owner in that conversation starts none, and the reaction is then not recorded: it is skipped, not queued, because the owner's turn already shows the member at work and an answer to a reaction would come after it, out of its place (analyses of reactions wait on each other, and never count as work for the owner). Never analysed: the owner's own message, the member's own answer to a reaction, a quick conversation, a reply that did not finish. In a group room only the reacted message's own author thinks, in the same thread, through the room's own speech turn (no other member, handoff or @), and not while the thread is being discussed; what it makes is no discussion of the room (not its meeting, not what it is busy with, and Stop wraps nothing up for it). A reaction is metadata, never approval and never the owner's words: no card, draft or question is settled by it, and the analysis is neither a note for the memory nor an instruction. | pytest, vitest, look | hard | core/tests/agent/test_emoji_reactions.py::test_a_message_carries_one_reaction_choosing_another_replaces_it_and_the_same_again_takes_it_back; ## Where a slime moves, and a screen that never flickers

A screen never flickers or jumps when a caption or other text updates. This section supersedes every place in R-UI-74, R-UI-99 and R-UI-78 that lets a slime move in the header, the panel, a card, a group, a dialog or the guide.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-SLIME-01 | 2026-11-11 | A slime moves in exactly two places: a member's row (or pinned tile) in the left member list, while that member works, and the thinking or waiting line of the open conversation (a private thread or a group). Everywhere else it is a picture of that slime: the conversation header, the details panel, the member settings page, group faces and their header cluster, hand-off and other cards, the new-member maker and the slime picker, mentions, dialogs, the guide's slimes, the computer's boot face and the search. A picture keeps its colour, hat, crown and accessories and the pose of rest (idle, or sleeping after a day's silence); it has no CSS animation, no transition, no strips, no clock and no state to hand over, and nothing observes it. `faceHTML` and `slimeFaceHTML` draw a picture unless they are given `moving`, and only the member row, the pinned tile and the thinking line give it. At a compact width the list is a drawer: while it is closed no slime is drawn there, so the look check asks for a moving one in the list only while the list is on screen. | vitest, look | hard | app/src/main/slime.test.ts::a slime is a picture unless it moves: whatever its member does, it keeps its resting pose and no clock; app/src/main/slime.test.ts::a picture keeps the states that are poses without motion, asleep and at rest, and a wait for the owner is only a slime at rest; app/src/main/slime.test.ts::faceHTML moves only when asked, for Raphael and for a member alike; app/src/main/slime.test.ts::the pictures of the picker, the maker and the stores never move; app/src/main/slime.test.ts::slime.css holds a picture still against every rule that could move it; app/src/main/ui-patch.test.ts::a picture of a slime is kept as it is by a redraw, and another member takes a fresh one; app/src/main/slime-offscreen.test.ts::a picture of a slime is never watched, so it costs the page nothing; app/scripts/look/slime-places.test.mjs::a slime in the member list or the thinking line may move, and one anywhere else must be a picture; app/scripts/look/slime-places.test.mjs::a list that is not on screen is not asked to move, and one that is on screen is; slime-places; slime-places-settings |
| R-SLIME-02 | 2026-11-11 | An update from the core touches only what changed. A member's step from one activity to another, or new words for what it is doing, change neither the identity of a thread line nor its face: a moving face is left out of a line's identity and the face already on screen is handed over by `setSlimeState` and `followSlimeRoster`, so the turn is not replaced, its words are not drawn again and nothing restarts. The conversations in the panel, the chip of the model and the model list are patched or left alone, never written anew, and the panel and the settings page do not draw again for a member's last line, time, unread mark or activity, nor does such a roster start a read of the panel's Library; a member's skills and commands are read when the slash menu is wanted, not at every roster event. The strips of a moving face (some thousands of nodes for a thinking or searching one) go in a slice of about 200 nodes a frame, out of sight, and show together, so that a change of state never holds a frame for 50 ms. A read that fails keeps what is on screen. | vitest, look | hard | app/src/main/thread-diff.test.ts::a line keeps its identity when only the state or the words of a member's face change; app/src/main/slime.test.ts::a roster read hands a face on screen over before the redraw, so the redraw keeps it; app/src/main/conversation-list.test.ts::a refresh patches the rows already on screen and a failed read keeps them; app/src/main/members.test.ts::a page about a member does not compare what changes with each word it says; app/src/main/pages/agent-members.test.ts::a roster that moves only a last line, a step or an unread mark starts no Library read and leaves the panel alone, and a renamed member reads and shows; app/src/main/slash-menu.test.ts::a member change reads nothing for a closed menu, and the next slash reads the skills it may have added; app/src/main/slime.test.ts::a heavy state's strips go in a slice a frame, out of sight, and show together, and a newer state drops the rest; app/scripts/look/event-storm.test.mjs::a storm that replaces a node, empties text, moves a scroll or animates a picture is told apart from a calm one; busy-team-calm |
| R-SLIME-03 | 2026-11-14 | A reply that queued research in the background once showed an empty slime under it and, under that, a task line with the slime and its progress words: two slimes. One member shows at most one working slime in one conversation, whatever the number of turns, tasks, cards and messages on their way. `standingPresence` (timeline.ts) is the one rule: of the turns that want a slime (`presenceWant`: `words` while a turn or its task works or its replies are being read in, `bare` while it waits for the owner at a card, a question, a yes or a waiting task, nothing once it is over) the newest one that works stands, so a background task's line with its progress words wins over an empty slime; a face that only waits stands only when nothing works, and then the newest of those. Every other turn draws none (`TurnLook.presenceTurnId`). What the owner has just sent or is saying (a pending bubble, a voice message) is newer than every turn and takes the slime itself: the line under the newest of them, carrying the working turn's words, and no turn draws its own beside it. A line's cached drawing knows whether it holds the slime, so a line that gives it up is drawn again. A group's thinking lines are per member and per message, not a thread (R-AG-02). | vitest, look | hard | app/src/main/standing-presence.test.ts::a turn wants words while it works, a bare face while it waits, and nothing once it is over; app/src/main/standing-presence.test.ts::two tasks: the newest stands, the older line draws none; app/src/main/standing-presence.test.ts::a task with words stands before a newer face that only waits; app/src/main/pages/agent-stop.test.ts::a reply that handed work to the background shows one slime, on the task line; app/src/main/pages/agent-stop.test.ts::a turn that is running without a task shows one slime; app/src/main/pages/agent-stop.test.ts::two background tasks of one member show one slime, on the newest; app/src/main/pages/agent-stop.test.ts::a task line with words wins over a face that only waits, as in the owner's picture; app/src/main/pages/agent-stop.test.ts::a message just sent takes the one slime from a task that waits; slime-one-working |

## One row at the top of a conversation

The top of a conversation is one row, not two, and the thread passes under that row.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-TOP-01 | 2026-11-12 | A conversation, a member's and a group's, has one row above its thread, and it is the window's own top row: the header's pill and its round buttons are centred 30 px from the window's top, with no empty strip above them. The tools at its right end 14 px short of the window's three buttons where the pane reaches the window's edge; where another pane stands at the right (the details panel, a group's side thread) that pane's own head is the row over it, carries the window's buttons, and the conversation's tools go to their pane's edge. On a Mac, where macOS draws its traffic lights at the top left, the right end keeps no room for buttons: the tools keep the pane's own inset, at least 16 px clear of the window's edge, and the details panel's last button ends its padding (16 px) short of the edge, as its first starts at the left. At narrow widths the sidebar is the rail of faces (R-SIDE-01), so the row starts at the pane's own edge: no menu button stands at the window's top left. The thread passes under the row: its first message keeps a top padding equal to the row, and what scrolls up fades out as it reaches the row (the thread's mask, a report's, nothing new), while the pill and the buttons stay on their own opaque surfaces. The row's empty parts still move the window and maximise it, and a wheel over the row still moves the thread; the pill and the buttons still press. The search bar, a page or a report open in the thread's place, and the conversation between members sit under or in place of the row without moving it. Every other page keeps its place under the window's strip. | vitest, look | hard | app/src/main/window-frame.test.ts::the row is 60 px, so the pill's centre stands 30 px from the window's top, and the buttons' cells are the width the tools stay clear of; app/src/main/window-frame.test.ts::on a Mac the row's right end keeps no room for caption buttons, which macOS draws at the top left, only the panel's padding; app/src/main/window-frame.test.ts::the header takes no room from the thread, which has no strip above it and passes under the row; app/src/main/window-frame.test.ts::the tools end short of the window's buttons, and go to their pane's edge where the details panel or a side thread stands beside them; app/src/main/window-frame.test.ts::the row's empty parts move the window, and the strip's own drag cell steps aside over it so that the pill and the buttons press; app/src/main/conversation-header-drag.test.ts::the empty parts of a conversation's header move the window and a double press maximises it once, while its pill and buttons are not taken; app/src/main/conversation-scroll.test.ts::a wheel turned over the header moves the thread under it, or the report open in its place, and leaves a zoom alone; app/scripts/look/checks.mjs::conversation-top-row (scenes top-row-find, top-row-details, top-row-room, top-row-room-find, top-row-scrolled, top-row-wheel, chat, conversation-share, details, raphael-details, room, calm-thread, page-inplace, page-inplace-back, page-inplace-room, page-report, page-report-share, room-thread-open) |

## Designed for everyone, not for one person

The product is designed for everyone, not for one person. A first-time-user audit (a fresh install: no key, no subscription, no connected app) found words, paths and defaults that only made sense to a long-time user. Only what moves no existing control is changed; the rest is open (the usage page and its limit, the start of the guide, the computer tab's wallpaper, one place for the dictation shortcut, the Composio and Connect Apps flows).

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-COPY-01 | 2026-11-16 | A message that sends the user to 「設定 › …」 names a place Settings has today: one of its eight tabs (一般, 模型, 用量, 工具, 團隊技能, 聽寫與快捷鍵, 安全, 關於) and what that tab draws (for 工具: 服務 and its four rows; for 模型: the model rows, 訂閱帳號 and 供應商; for 用量: 概覽 and 分析; for 聽寫與快捷鍵: its sections, the languages and the microphone). Apps are connected in the sidebar's 「連結應用程式」, a member's messaging is in that member's details and the local-command question is asked on its card, so none of them is written as a Settings path. The Composio website's own 「Settings › Project Settings › API Keys」 is the site's, in its English. The words for Settings are one per language. The test reads every catalogue (fifteen languages), the core's Python (failures, persona, the words of messaging and members, the tables of `_ui_words_data.py`) and the shell's notices (`notices.rs`, `notice_words.rs`), and fails on a path to a place that is not there. | vitest | hard | app/src/main/settings-paths.test.ts::every message of every catalogue; app/src/main/settings-paths.test.ts::every message of the core and the shell; app/src/main/settings-paths.test.ts::the checker knows a place that is not there; app/src/main/settings-paths.test.ts::the tabs of the tree are the tabs of Settings, in every language |
| R-COPY-02 | 2026-11-18 | An OpenRouter key is one provider's among the others: it is added, tested, replaced and removed on OpenRouter's card in Settings › 模型 › 供應商 as any provider's key is, and 用量 has no OpenRouter section and asks for no key, with one or without (it showed a paste field to everyone without OpenRouter, which most people never use). The guide's AI tools have no OpenRouter key tile: OpenRouter is a gateway tile opening the same editor, and 熱門 holds the accounts and official keys most people use. The dictation notice asks for a model only when none can tidy (`status.get`'s `cleanup_ready`, whichever provider the model has), names no provider, and opens 模型; a key the owner set that fails is still told. It stands on the dictation page only: the conversation shows nothing about dictation on its own. A provider's card has no switch: it opens the provider's editor, and one turned off in an earlier version says 已停用 with 啟用. A feature used with no model, or with a model whose source is off, says which feature in the window's language and never a provider's key (`persona.py`, `models.missing` → `ModelMissing`, at most once a minute), dictation pastes the words as spoken, and the toast's one button opens that feature's choice. A choice whose source is off or signed out stays shown on its own row and card and says 「{source} 未連上」 in the warning colour. The speech models and the catalogue read while Settings is closed are kept and drawn when it opens, so the local list never stays 正在載入.
